ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e10377	Madam President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Citizens! Also from my side: Dear Lena Jäger, your team and all the volunteers' warm congratulations to this popular desire, which was a huge success in the second edition! 282,000 signatorys represent the last step towards an equal society, equal rights between women and men, resulting in a better situation for all. I would like to respond to the following demand:'spent reduction of working time to 30 hours per week at variable wage and staff compensation'. – This is a contemporary demand, which is being countercared by the current government policy; with the 60-hour week, we have catapulted Austria back into the last century. In the course of digitalisation, we experience that markets do not grow but become more efficient. We can assume that in ten to 20 years, many professions will be replaced by robots, artificial intelligence, automation and computer programs. Future research speaks of 40 to 60 percent loss of jobs due to this development. In addition, women today still vote two thirds of unpaid care and housework and are therefore very often forced into temporary and low paid part-time work. About a third of these women are now vulnerable to poverty. Fair and family-friendly are such working conditions for no one, and they are not much more to argue in national economic terms. We must take these developments seriously – digitisation, automation – and develop a progressive labour policy and forward-looking work models in symbiosis with digitization. Therefore, the unconditional basic income is not a question of preferences and options, but a inevitable consequence. What's that got to do with women? – A lot. A suitable quote from Mr Mathis Hampel, science researcher from Vienna: [[Abg. Lasar: Who is this?]] If in a society with basic incomes all have more opportunities, then those who have the least possible today have the most of them, and that is very often women. - Quote. Moreover, this is a very underestimated aspect: If we share the share of wealth and power between women and men the same way, we will move much faster into the important, essential issues of the future, such as social justice and climate protection. The fact that climate change is not just about CO2 footprints has recognized Pope Francis’ encyclical “Laudato si” in his climate cycle. On 200 pages he mentions CO2 five times, climate twelve times, dignity 25 times and poverty 59 times. The Pope raises the issue of distribution and links climate change with social equality – and thus we are again at the 30-hour week and the women's people's desire. With the unconditional basic income, financially paid work would be abolished and a work and life in dignity for all would be possible. Whoever is ill treated, who does not find any meaning in his work, he does not work, and from this he derives most of the social problems. The basic income at the same time frees policy from the doctrine, the hostage of full employment at all costs, because the Forced full employment brings us titles like Fossil of the Day in Katowice. This negative price is awarded daily to countries which are counterproductive in the negotiations during the World Climate Conference. If we manage to interpret the signs of time properly, we do not ask ourselves how we can best combat climate change, but we ask ourselves: What can climate change do for us? It cannot be less than the positive change in all our favors at all levels. The fear of income from employment hinders effective climate protection, the fear of income from employment threatens social peace and hinders any kind of development in a society. A government that can't secure its population's existential income will be given Yellowwest's answer. In order for Austria to become France, we need the unconditional basic income in the medium term and on the way there the reduction of working time and the 30-hour week, all of which benefit in the country, not just the women. The boys started smoking in the circle of friends and tried out the first cigarette – one before they were 16 years old, the other later. It often doesn't stay while trying; Youths develop significantly different from adults, so also in dependence behaviour: Youths are much more vulnerable to dependence and addiction. The higher the age of smoker entry, the easier you can stop. That's why we raise the age of protection when smoking to 18 years. The best prevention against any addiction is undoubtedly to avoid entry. All other measures are not very promising and a later exit is simply more difficult. Smoking is harmful to health, we all know, but in particular smoking is harmful to children and young people who are in the middle of development. That is why these two years, which one protects longer from nicotine, are very important. From the statistics we know: Whom before the 18th. Life does not start smoking, in most cases remains a non-smoker. Austria is one of the last EU countries to allow smoking for under 18 years of age. Austria is also one of the EU countries in which most young people smoke. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Haubner.]] Then some say: Well, that doesn't work, because young people under 16 are already smoking, although they shouldn't! What then brings the increase to 18 years at all? – International studies show, however, that raising the age of protection is very likely to have an effect; there is a decrease in teen smokers. That is logical, too, because many have their cigarettes out of the circle of friends in young years, from the older ones who already have a corresponding ID card. This will at least be difficult in the future. Of course, there will continue to be young people who smoke before they are 18 years old. – I’m so realistic, that’s quite clear. However, I have said that the first cigarette is smoked in Austria at about 12 years of age. If we can also raise this actual age by two years by increasing the legal age of protection, then a great step will be taken to protect our children and young people. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Many here, especially my colleagues as youth speakers, stand for a uniform protection of young people in Austria instead of nine different national laws. Our federal states have become known for a common way and are also gradually implementing smoking protection for up to 18 years. This aspect is often neglected in the smoking debate, although it is precisely these measures which protect our youth that will bring great progress and reduce the number of addicts in the long term. - Thank you.

In order to answer the question properly and forward, we must probably also draw lessons from the past development. Why is there public broadcasting? – Because after the end of the Second World War, the British model of radio and TV institutions, which belong to themselves and thus all residents of a country, prevailed. That was the answer to the state radio and to the people's receivers, and it was the answer to propaganda and hatred. Whoever wasn't sure if this was so good, right and well, he knew it at the latest in Prague spring. It was the voice of the Austrian radio that opposed: a voice against tyranny and against state radio, and it is, as it were, legend how well the ORF worked at that time and how important it is to have an independent radio. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] All representatives of the Political basics and the idea of liberal democracy will support this idea. While the age group of over 60-year-olds consume 97 percent of their content via linear television, when we speak of moving image, the 14- to 29-year-olds are only more than 66 percent, and in the current TV program, the ratio is 92 to 48 percent. Less than half of the moving content comes from the boys over the current TV program, already 10 percent over YouTube. And while you were still sitting in a sad round in front of the TV in the 1980s on Saturday evening, television for young people today, in public places, in their rooms, in the subway on the phone. So if we want our values not only to continue the media revolution but also to co-form it, we must confess to this system, and that means remembering the principles of the foundation that would be there: Firstly, independence in financing must be ensured. The funding from the budget should be rejected as well as a purely content promotion of public value content. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Secondly, only funding by way of fees ensures the independence of the ORF as much as possible. According to the model of Great Britain, I believe that the flow-infalls should be concluded anyway. Of course – I share Karl Nehammer’s view, which is rarely the case in itself; he may be executed when he comes back – but the public-law order has to be redefined. [[Intervocations at the ÖVP]] Public broadcasting must prove its legitimacy on a daily basis. Sports and quality entertainment are also part of it as well as art, culture and information. We need, and we all know, a comprehensive digitalisation strategy for the ORF. It must find a connection with the needs of young people, must be visible on all platforms. A content platform should be developed together with private providers, and the 7-day catch-up rule should be lifted. Journalistic quality and independence must continue to be subject to effective self-control, control by the Foundation Council, but also by the Media Authority. And – long ago due from my point of view – it needs uniform GIS contributions for all of Austria. It is not to be seen that in a country of different GIS charges are paid by the size of Austria. Thus, for example, proverbs and slogans can be transported on transparents unhindered, so that Ustasha symbols can be carried unhindered, so loud Ustasha songs are sung and the Ustasha greeting prohibited in Croatia is always shown on site. All of this can take place under the protection of this ecclesial event. This also makes it clear that we urgently need measures to be able to become master of this situation and to intervene directly against these symbols and to be able to take action against the presentation and wearing of these symbols. The 1960 badge law places the wearing and displaying of badges, uniforms and uniforms of organisations prohibited by the Prohibition Act in a way that prohibits and prohibits them. Our application for the signage law is intended to extend this application for foreign organisations that have cooperated organisationally and in terms of content with these prohibited organisations and to include uniforms, badges and the like of these organisations. This would mean, on the one hand, that we have concrete measures and controls to take against the Ustasha, but in that case it also includes, for example, the Hungarian arrow cruisers, the Spanish Franco fascists or the Italian Mussolini fascists. Now to the symbol law: In principle, we welcome the fact that the Ustasha is also mentioned in the symbol law. We think it is good and right that we want to take measures to combat organisations and groups such as the grey wolves or the Muslim Brotherhood. However, and this also shows the opinions that have been given to this law – in the first place that of the Federal Chamber of Labour – it is not clear to us how this list of organizations to which the symbol law is to be extended has come about. It just appears arbitrary. There are no limits to what has been drawn. We have also mentioned this in the Committee on the Interior, Minister for the Interior. You referred us to the Subcommittee in answer to this question. However, this will not take place until next week, and we cannot, so to speak, locate why you have specifically listed these organizations and not others. We are dealing with specific organizations. What about the far right identities? What about the refusals? What about the People's Party? For example, why are these not on this list? [[Congratulations with SPÖ and NOW.]] What we can't estimate, either, because this list is apparently not yet available, is what symbols it really is about. Which symbols and gestures should be specifically listed? You can or could not provide us with any relevant information. It should also be borne in mind that, as soon as these organisations change their symbols, this law or the regulation on this law must be constantly extended and amended. The symbol law was decided in 2015 to take action against the Islamic State and Al Qaida. We have also asked whether there is already an evaluation and whether one can say whether this law is effective accordingly. You said that this evaluation will only take place after five years, i.e. in 2020. I have a bit of fear that it could be merely a symbolic symbol law, because it will probably be difficult in implementation and execution. We have also accepted this in different opinions. All these reasons and, above all, the arbitrary nature of this list make us unable to agree to this symbol law today, because we recognize this arbitraryity in this list. If it works, we'll see in the middle of May next year. Then it will be shown whether the Ustasha meeting will take place in the form as it has now happened, either in 2019 or whether the symbol law acts as a concrete measure. – In this sense, thank you very much.

This is less a political measure than a solidarity for the benefit of people. I think that the situation is undisputed. Lightenness: This is how you can be deceived!]] All the more astonished I was that at the meeting of the Committee on Internal Affairs, exactly this rejection occurred. Point Report of the Committee on Internal Affairs on the Government Template (380, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Civil Service Act 1986 (422 d.a.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e19542	We have thus reached item 9. There was no oral reporting. As First of all, Mr Werner Herbert. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e19573	Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) We are talking here about the federal law which amends the Civil Service Act. What is this essentially about? The aim is to increase the attractiveness of civil service, to spread, on the one hand, through administrative simplification and, on the other hand, through improvements to civil service providers. The aim is to take into account the wishes and needs of the institutions, but also the concerns of the administrative implementation and to make improvements in this regard. And it is about improving the possibilities for participation and control both from the federal and from the country side. There are numerous good and important improvements in this law. Let me tell you a few: These are the introduction of a computerised training module for civil service staff, but also for seniors in civil service facilities, including a corresponding certification; the addition of the conditions for the recognition and withdrawal of the recognition of civil service facilities; enhanced participation rights by the Federal Minister for Internal Affairs and the Civil Service Agency; the possibility of the head of state to reduce the approved maximum number of seats at work; the early release of civilian service workers in the event of a total of 24 calendar days' sickness – that was not possible in this form until now, but only if there is a continuous absence of 18 days –; the obligation to notify the civil service provider without delay in the event of a health damage as a result of civil service, but also the possibility of a second application for the discharge of civil service duty for 12 months. This is relevant when it comes to the fact that the civil servant would like to make a professional choice as a member of a guard and that this quasiv extension has only been possible by delay – such as a new, improved, administratively clean solution. We know that civil service is an important, indispensable part in the social services sector of the state and the countries. With this government template, we ensure that, on the one hand, the quality standard in the civil service sector, but also the rights and obligations of civil servants, as well as the rights and obligations of the authorities are clearly regulated. We are also fulfilling an approach, a promise that we have made in the government programme. I think that this is a good law, a good one, an important one, a meaningful extension of the previous possibilities to ensure that civil service remains attractive on the one hand in the future and, on the other hand, civil service in the current expression also appears to be secure for future generations. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e19612	Mr Konrad Antoni is next speaking. - Please.

However, I would like to say quite explicitly, as I have already done in the Committee, that civil service is a military service, and so in many areas I see civil service as essential support, as an additional force, of course, in support of fixed employees in many areas of the social, safety and health system. Point Report of the Committee on Internal Affairs on the Government Template (369, e.g._Other Organiser The Federal Act establishing the Federal Law to ensure a high level of security of network and information systems (NISG) and amending the Telecommunications Act 2003 (418 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e20117	This leads us to the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr David Lasar came to speak. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e20148	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! Mr. State Secretary! The Network and Information System Security Act, the so-called NIS Act, is to be amended. Cybersecurity, ladies and gentlemen, is one of the most important priorities of the Federal Government. The aim is to establish uniform cybersecurity standards for companies and federal bodies in the fields of energy, transport, banking, financial markets, health care, drinking water supply, digital infrastructure and digital service providers. The law is based on the so-called NIS directives and on European legislation. In order to enable the companies concerned to adapt the IT systems, there will be a sufficiently long transitional period. The law takes an important step towards ensuring a high cybersecurity level in Austria. This is done by the need to protect businesses of critical infrastructure, whose services depend on citizens in their everyday lives, as well as important digital service providers and the Federal Government to protect their digital systems from attacks in the future. In order to make the law safe for the future and to ensure a practical regulation, a close dialogue with the experts in this field has also been held out of the economy. The aim was to enable efficient implementation and to avoid double-tracking. The essential services are also to be evaluated every two years in order to ensure future security. The BKA has already negotiated the NIS Directive for Austria in the EU and, in this sense, is also observing the strategic agendas at EU level. The BMI, in turn, has many years of experience with the technical treatment of security incidents, which is why the operational agendas are bundled there. An accurate analysis has also been carried out of what tasks Austria needs to perform under the NIS Directives in the future and to what extent it is possible to use existing resources in implementation. The Federal Government has also set itself, as stated in the government programme, the goal of protecting citizens from the dangers and risks associated with the progressive digitalisation and of making digital security gaps in Austria as best possible. I think this law is an important step in the right direction, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e20301	Madam President. Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! Mr State Secretary! The two previous speakers have already mentioned that this law is about ensuring a high level of safety, especially in network and information systems. In principle, it is about creating criteria and procedures to protect society, the economy or the state from cyberattacks. We have already heard, as essential services, issues have been defined in the following areas: Electricity, energy, healthcare – you think of hospital, health care – as well as the transport systems – from the conventional transport system of cars, trucks, roads to shipping or the flight system. Banking and Internet services are also included as essential services. I think cybercrime has already become a concept in the population in the meantime. When we look at the annual security report, we note that the number of cases of Internet-related crime is rising year after year. We are talking about both the simple spam mail that promises an unexpected profit and at the same time is pummeling money or trying to get your own bank data, access data, as well as malware, such as a Ransomware like WannaCry, which caused massive damage to individuals, businesses and the public sector a few months ago, because it infects numerous computers, encrypts important data and then extorts money to get back to the data. But of course it is also so far that attacks are intentionally made to paralyze infrastructure, for example through a DDoS attack. As I said, no one is right before that. It affects both private and businessmen as well as the state. If we look at the incidents of recent years, we see that is quite serious. Only in November has it become known that in Germany, members of the Bundestag, but also the government, were exposed to attacks by hackers and important data were stolen. In the course of the WannaCry attacks a few months ago, for example, the Deutsche Bahn became victims. Many computers have been paralysed, display boards, but also the ticket machines have no longer worked. In hospitals too, it has already been the subject of the fact that important data has been encrypted. Imagine that patients should be treated and that data are no longer available. As I said, even in Austria this is not an unknown phenomenon. The only thing to think of is the airport Schwechat, which was exposed to hacker attacks from Turkey some time ago. One sees that this is a matter of vital importance, and it will become an even more important one in the future, especially in the increasing networking of devices, the Internet of Things. From this point of view, the issue of security, namely network and infrastructure security, as well as the protection of the data to be found on it must always be considered. But I also say: A 100% protection is never possible. It's actually like a cat and mouse game. At each update, vulnerabilities are closed, and at the same time hackers or other organizations find new ones again. What we want to achieve in any case with this law is greater cooperation, an exchange of information between companies, between the organisations concerned and the authorities themselves, because if important information is exchanged, one can also help each other. If an attack has become known somewhere, you can provide assistance to others, analyze security incidents and risks, and take measures so that you do not become a victim. I believe that overall the proposal is a good implementation – a warm thanks from this side goes to the Ministry of the Interior under today's leadership, but also under Minister Hanni Mikl-Leiter and Minister Sobotka, who have done a great deal of preparatory work and have already gathered important insights into how to protect cybersecurity in Austria, as part of the preparatory work in the sense of planning games with various companies and organisations. – Thank you very much for that!

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e23363	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! With the IFI contribution law, we do five different international financial institutions either with replenishments or with capital increases, and we will also agree. These five instruments cover a very wide range of relevant funding for developing and emerging countries. This goes from combating poverty to promoting the economy and infrastructure, an economy that is sustainable when it really comes to people and not only has a few of them, but it also creates good and paid and safe jobs. On the other hand, environmental issues are addressed. Especially many African countries suffer from the consequences of climate change, and since adaptation measures and other measures are to be taken, it is only in the concert that really big instruments go. This can hardly be achieved with bilateral assistance. In addition, for example, specific investments are also being made in food production. This is not only relevant and important in terms of the food security and food sovereignty of the countries concerned, but also important because, for example, rural areas in the countries of the global south, where there is a lot of rural flight, have a lot of internal migration, in so far as life prospects are being strengthened there. These are really important and meaningful activities and contributions that are being made. I would also like to say that by the year 2028, a total of EUR 153 million will be invested from Austria, including a contribution to achieving the sustainable development goals of the United Nations Agenda 2030, which addresses many issues, from poverty reduction to rural development. These funds are also relevant as regards the Austrian ODA, the public development payments, even though I have to say that the multilateral instruments from the Ministry of Finance make a relatively large contribution. Where there really is to be found is bilateral development cooperation. We are currently at an ODA quota of 0.3 percent. I thank you at this point in the budget service for having exchanged the figures in the report on this law on the website for the current 2017 figures. However, as we are supposed to achieve with a forecast of 0.24 percent in 2022, each time, what the government has set out in its government programme, namely a commitment to an increase in the direction of 0.7 percent, is probably not only a mystery to me, but many others in this area as well. But whatever it is: These contributions to the international financial institutions are important, meaningful and sustainable, and we will also agree with them. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e23424	Thank you, Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Dear Members! Dear colleagues! I would like to say a few words today about amending the Civil Fund law. It is primarily about helping the municipality of Gasen in Styria. The municipality of Gasen in the Styria has been surprised four times by bad floods in the last two years alone, has suffered enormous damage, and there have even been two deaths in early 2000. The existing cultivation measures are no longer sufficient, and it is therefore necessary to invest a total of around EUR 13.5 million. The municipality's share is 3.2 million euros. For a community with not even 1 000 inhabitants, this is an almost indispensable amount, which is why I believe it is very important to take this decision here today, so that the installation can be started very quickly, so that safety can be created on the one hand and, on the other hand, psychological assistance for those affected by the construction process. If you live there and have to be afraid of every thunderstormer, you know what it means when installation measures are used: that safety comes. I assume that this decision will be taken unanimously today. I would like to look back on how it happened to us in my home community in 2016. We were also captured twice by a very bad wall, 40 houses were shed. At that time, it was natural for all the members of the state to let help grow; whether the Red Head of State, of course also the Blue Councillor Köfer, even the Green Council of State has been there and has helped out of his department. Looking back, I must say that I was somewhat surprised today that at the agenda item on mutual assistance with Russia in the case of natural disasters suddenly No, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, When it comes to helping people, there must be no barriers in the minds. In Carinthia it worked, in the Styria it works, but apparently not quite here.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e23464	The next item is Mrs Yildirim's speech. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-11-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00053_d7e23485	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen, I want to lose a few words on the Financial Balance Act. The Center for Administrative Research described the task orientation in 2017 as a central reform measure to the Financial Balance Act, and I think we have suffered a step backwards with the abolition of § 15. I think this is very unfortunate, because remembering that tax revenues are to be distributed between the federal, state and local authorities makes it absolutely sense to link these distributed funds to a specific purpose. It surprises me all the more, because we often hear from ÖVP or FPÖ series: more money for more performance instead of distributing money according to the foundry principle. The implementation of the task orientation was an opportunity to strengthen support for those communities that carry out tasks beyond their municipal boundaries. I would also like to briefly present this in the example of Tyrol. In Tyrol – if we stay in elementary education, i.e. in the care of children until the sixth year of life, which also happened at § 15 – it is the fact that the kindergartens have closed on 42,5 days a year. To make it even clearer: The proportion of kindergartens in Tyrol, which have opened less than 8 hours a day, is 54.4 percent. A task orientation would be a It was a chance to give Tyrolean communities the opportunity to spend more money across the community. So it doesn't necessarily have to be that conurbation centres or major cities benefit more, but it will be very dependent on the project. It has been stressed so often that the compatibility of work and family is so important. However, with the departure from planning security, you have made that very difficult. That is to say, the communities that want to meet the desire to make work and family compatible have taken planning security as well as the children affected and their parents. And it is not to be seen that there is such an inequality. In this sense, I think that the communities which have more and more tasks to do should not be supported in accordance with the principle of foundry, but more strongly after actual performance. It was – admittedly – a punctual measure, but it was a real opportunity to make a more modern and just society possible. We very much regret that this paragraph has been abolished, and this is also the reason why we will not agree to this revision of the Financial Balance Act.

We know that the financial scandal, which was caused by the SPÖ members in the state of Salzburg at the time, has caused massive damage. Now this federal government is helping, and I am right to thank you very much. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Third point: Today, we are adopting an agreement between the Republic of Austria and the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland to avoid double taxation and to prevent the reduction of taxes. There is already an agreement from 1969, but this is no longer in line with the latest developments in international tax law. That is why it is necessary for us to adopt a new agreement today, which has now been signed, but we also have to confirm it in Parliament. The objectives of this new agreement are to strengthen the location of Austria and to further strengthen economic relations, including with a view to the leaving of Great Britain from the European Union. Economic relations between Austria and the UK are intense, and we want to continue and intensify this in the future. I should like to point out once again that it is about avoiding double taxation and preventing tax reduction and improving dispute resolution and increasing legal certainty for the companies concerned. It is a good agreement, therefore: Voices You agree to this agreement!

A few words on the Financial Balance Act later on: This is an Article 15a agreement – which has already been mentioned several times – on elementary pedagogy, support for the federal government. The objectives formulated by our budget service are: to strengthen basic educational institutions, to improve the compatibility of family and work, to provide a year-round full-day care provision for children up to school entry, to improve the educational opportunities of children regardless of their socio-economic origin through pre-school support, to increase early linguistic support. That is the objective of this agreement. This agreement has preceded a hack with the countries, especially with the socially democratically-led countries. Now you need to know that the responsibility for this elementary pedagogy, speak kindergarten care or kindergarten guidance, lies with the communities. The question has also been raised several times today, and I am, in particular, also with Mr Rossmann: What does it need for the countries at all? I can tell you, Mr Rossmann: The countries obviously need it because, with this money that you get from the federal government – and that is about 140 million euros a year – you are unfortunately making party-political small money. And unfortunately, our federal state with Mr. Landeshuptmann Kaiser, the deputy of Mrs. Rendi-Wagner, is a negative example of this. What's the SPÖ doing in Carinthia? – With this money, she makes a special class money distribution campaign. As Mrs Yildirim mentioned it, so today she does not want to agree to the law, the SPÖ Carinthia applies the principle of foundry. Money will be distributed without there being only one care place or one hour more childcare in Carinthia. [[Abg. New builder: This is crazy!]] It is simply transmitted directly to the parents of the contribution – no matter how the child care offer is. Kärntner Kinder-Stipendium calls that the SPÖ. This distribution action, which in the past has just been carried out by Ladies and gentlemen have been so criticized, now par excellence is implemented by SPÖ in Carinthia. As has already been said, responsibility for childcare lies with the communities, that is their task. I can give you an example of the practice, because in 2004 we completely changed childcare with us in the municipality, since then we have run an age-wide kindergarten. Children from one and a half years of age are admitted, there are afternoon care for children up to 15 years of age. And since then, the kindergarten has been used up to 100 percent. We operate it all day, we operate it all year, we closed it in summer only two weeks. That's the offer the parents need today. This has also been mentioned: Especially in rural areas, working parents need full-day childcare, from an age of one and a half years. Only: Where does the money come from now? – We receive about 50 percent of this cost through the country’s funding and parental contributions. A babysitting centre costs us around 5 000 euros over the year. And 2 500 euros is currently financing the municipality. This money, which is now distributed in Carinthia, will no longer create a single care facility, will not increase the supply of care in the communities because the funds will be distributed with the casting can. It is a pity that this money, which the League makes available, is so distributed. It has already been mentioned today that this would be something for a real federalism reform, which one would like to address immediately. And I would agree here as a concerned mayor and as a national council, only one must finally get the countries into the boat, because the countries do not need to be on this matter, you can take it out. It would be an easy thing, as the Finance Minister Schelling, now Finance Minister Löger and this Federal Government have already tried to do, that is to make the money available to the municipalities directly according to the services offered by the municipality. Then we would have the care, the childcare, of which all are talking but unfortunately will not become reality, because the money just doesn't come where it has to come, namely to the relevant communities. - Thank you.
